- **problem-doc-model-selector**: 解析赛题 PDF 或 Word 或题面文本，抽取每一问的任务、数据条件与约束，并复核公式、图片与分问标题的提取完整性。当用户提供赛题文档、需要先明确题意与分问、或题面提取结果待人工复核时使用。 ([yushui2022/MathModel-Skill](https://github.com/yushui2022/MathModel-Skill/tree/HEAD/skills/problem-doc-model-selector) · ★463)
- **problem-doc-model-selector**: 解析赛题PDF/Word，抽取任务与数据条件并给出模型选型与验证路线。Invoke when用户提供赛题文档或题目文本，需要确定采用何模型/方法。 ([xzwwwwww/Enhanced-mathmodel-Codex-skills](https://github.com/xzwwwwww/Enhanced-mathmodel-Codex-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/problem-doc-model-selector) · ★113 · 3 copies)

- **problem-driven-learning-loop**: 当需要高效学习新技能或启动新项目，避免陷入空想或无效学习时 ([DracoUnion/dankoe-skills](https://github.com/DracoUnion/dankoe-skills/tree/HEAD/problem-driven-learning-loop) · ★14)

([yujie-jason-zhang/polish_skill](https://github.com/yujie-jason-zhang/polish_skill/tree/HEAD/problem-driven-literature-review) · ★26 · 2 copies)

- **problem-editorial**: Cria, completa ou melhora um editorial didático de programação competitiva a partir de um enunciado em PDF, opcionalmente com vídeo, transcrição ou esboço Markdown. - **problem-evidence**: Use when asking how solid the evidence is behind a candidate problem or statement, what evidence is missing, or what the cheapest next evidence would be ('how solid is the evidence behind P-003?', 'what are we missing for P-001?'). Not for finding new problems in raw material — use problem-discovery. - **problem-evidence**: Use for actionable CREATE work requiring accepted problem/discovery evidence. ([lehater/harness](https://github.com/lehater/harness/tree/HEAD/skills/artifacts/problem-evidence))
- **problem-exploration**: Use when facing a technical problem with multiple possible solutions, or when a previous approach failed and you need to reason about alternatives. Structures the space of options before committing to any one. Core output: a brainstorm log with options, tradeoffs, a chosen approach, and unresolved gaps that must be answered before implementation starts. Use before experiment-driven-development Step 4 when the design space is large or uncertain. ([Victoriakaey/build-reliable-agents](https://github.com/Victoriakaey/build-reliable-agents/tree/HEAD/skills/problem-exploration))
- **problem-explore**: Investigate solution spaces for hackathon challenges after problem reframing. Use when a brief or problem-reframing.md exists and the team needs evidence-backed, non-obvious solution approaches before architecture selection. Coordinates independent research subagents, contradiction analysis, feasibility checks, and selective deepening. ([Yertugan06/Cash-discipline-video-checker](https://github.com/Yertugan06/Cash-discipline-video-checker/tree/HEAD/archive/skills/problem-explore) · 2 copies)
- **problem-factories**: Routes built-in optimizee problem selection, data-backed problem setup, custom loss factories, and util.get_config mappings. ([VectorSpaceLab/AREX-Skill](https://github.com/VectorSpaceLab/AREX-Skill/tree/HEAD/skills/repositories/repo-skills/learning-to-learn/sub-skills/problem-factories) · ★357 · 2 copies)
- **problem-finder**: Force a problem-discovery pass before any solutioning. Decompose startup and hackathon ideas into the worker, their current workaround, and the structural gap that keeps the pain unfixed; reject obvious, feel-safe framings by default. Use when the user mentions a startup idea, hackathon idea, "what should I build," validating an idea, finding problems, user pain points, or presents any solution without a validated problem — even if they do not ask for problem discovery explicitly. ([fozagtx/problem-finder](https://github.com/fozagtx/problem-finder/blob/HEAD/SKILL.md) · ★1 · 4 copies)
- **problem-firewall**: Use when the user wants help solving something but the problem is vague, symptom-level, or framed as a premature solution. Enforce a problem-solution firewall: clarify the desired outcome, obstacles, root-cause condition, and user-owned plain-language Problem Frame before moving to solution methods. ([Ivcota/skills](https://github.com/Ivcota/skills/tree/HEAD/learn-think/problem-firewall) · ★7)
- **problem-first**: use this when a team has locked onto a solution and needs pulling back to the problem: diagnose the solution jump, surface assumptions, write a problem statement, and propose validation before design starts ([Shixuuu/grokbot-pi-templates](https://github.com/Shixuuu/grokbot-pi-templates/tree/HEAD/templates/community/teams-handoffs/ai-pm-os/skills/problem-first) · ★6)
- **problem-first**: Ensures problem-first framing before solutioning. Use when defining work, discussing features, handling requests that sound like solutions, or when someone says "we should add" or "let's build". ([Elmmly/genie-team](https://github.com/Elmmly/genie-team/tree/HEAD/skills/problem-first) · ★3)
- **problem-first**: Define the real problem before any solution. Trigger 'problem first'. ([Pawel-Kica/ai-coding-100](https://github.com/Pawel-Kica/ai-coding-100/tree/HEAD/skills/thinking/problem-first) · ★3)
- **problem-first**: Inverts a proposed solution back into the problem underneath it. Use when a stakeholder, team, or roadmap hands you a solution ("we need a new notification system", "let's build X", "add a dashboard") and you need to find the real problem before committing build time — or when triaging a backlog of your own half-baked ideas to see which have a real problem and evidence behind them. Triggers on "problem-first", "what problem does this solve", "decompose this feature/idea", "triage my ideas", "is this the right thing to build", or a roadmap/feature/idea handed over as a fait accompli. ([oskarhane/oskars.ai](https://github.com/oskarhane/oskars.ai/tree/HEAD/plugins/product-skills/skills/problem-first) · ★1)
- **problem-first**: Rewrites a draft agent prompt into problem, evidence, ideas, autonomy and done criteria, so you hand over the problem instead of your solution. Use when drafting a prompt for an agent. Triggers on /problem-first and on phrases like 'rewrite this prompt problem-first', 'am I prescribing a fix', 'tighten this prompt before I send it'. ([evemcgivern/agent-skills](https://github.com/evemcgivern/agent-skills/tree/HEAD/plugins/agent-workflow/skills/problem-first))
- **problem-first-explanation**: Structures explanations of tools, methods, concepts, rules, and proposals by leading with the concrete problem. Use when explaining a solution; not for whole-document structure (use documentation). ([fabiandistler/agents](https://github.com/fabiandistler/agents/tree/HEAD/skills/problem-first-explanation) · ★2)
- **problem-first-goal-setting**: 当需要设定个人发展或转型目标，但感到迷茫或受外界影响时 ([DracoUnion/dankoe-skills](https://github.com/DracoUnion/dankoe-skills/tree/HEAD/problem-first-goal-setting) · ★14)
- **problem-first-opportunity-identification**: 当需要寻找真实的创业或产品创意，而非凭空想象时 ([DracoUnion/dankoe-skills](https://github.com/DracoUnion/dankoe-skills/tree/HEAD/problem-first-opportunity-identification) · ★14)
- **problem-first-prd**: Use when writing a spec for a feature or a one-way-door decision — a Working-Backwards PRD whose whole top half is WHY before any WHAT. ([wastedcode/truecast](https://github.com/wastedcode/truecast/tree/HEAD/personas/product-manager/core/skills/problem-first-prd) · ★8)
- **problem-first-teaching-notes**: Write student-ready teaching or lecture notes that build a concept, definition, method or theorem from the problem that motivates it, instead of stating a definition and proving properties. Use when the user asks for teaching notes, lecture notes, a handout, or to teach, present or explain a term or topic to students or an audience. ([Louis-Chenyang-Lu/problem-first-teaching-notes](https://github.com/Louis-Chenyang-Lu/problem-first-teaching-notes/tree/HEAD/skills/codex/problem-first-teaching-notes) · 2 copies)

- **problem-focus**: Use when narrowing a shortlist of candidate problems to the 2–3 to pursue now, with an explicit not-now list and revisit triggers ('which of these should we work on next quarter?', 'help me choose between P-001, P-005 and P-007'), including when asked to rank them or name the single most important one (it declines that and picks 2–3). - **problem-focus**: (startup-skills) Use after an idea passes pressure-test, or when the founder has a vague problem but no sharp hypothesis. Fires on "who is this for," "define my ICP," "companies need better X," or any abstract problem description. Drills recursively until the ICP is specific, locatable, and testable. Refuses to proceed if the ICP cannot be found online in concentrated form. - **problem-formalization-coach**: 当题面已经干净、但不知道该调用哪条物理定律，或者用了定律却说不清它在这里凭什么成立时使用。把问题拆成已知量、未知量、约束三栏，为每一条给定条件追出它背后的定律，并强制写明该定律在本题的适用条件；每条假设标出它近似掉了哪段物理；用未知数与独立方程的计数判断问题定不定得下来。检出凭空补进来的量和"用了定律但没写条件"的条目。不列出解、不推导、不给数值答案。 ([ChromiteCr/Skills](https://github.com/ChromiteCr/Skills/tree/HEAD/skills/physics/problem-formalization-coach) · ★5)
- **problem-formulation**: When the user wants to translate a word problem or real-world planning situation into a formal optimization model — identifying decision variables, objective function, and constraints, choosing the model type (LP, MIP, CP, or heuristic), estimating model size, and auditing the data before any solver code is written. Also use when the user mentions "formulate," "model this problem," "decision variables," "objective function," "translate to optimization," or when a problem arrives as plain prose with no mathematical structure yet. For implementing the resulting MIP, see milp-modeling-gurobi; fo… ([hajibabaie/combinatorial-optimization-skills](https://github.com/hajibabaie/combinatorial-optimization-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/problem-formulation) · ★18)
- **problem-frame**: Frame or revise literature-review questions. Use when a request needs a clear review object, scope, variables, constraints, or success criteria before literature review; when the user asks to research, investigate, improve, evaluate, compare, debug, or explain limitations of a method, system, benchmark, dataset, paper, or research direction through related work; or when the user corrects or challenges an existing frame. ([ustcllm/kaivu-agent-ts](https://github.com/ustcllm/kaivu-agent-ts/tree/HEAD/agent-adapters/codex/skills/problem-frame) · ★3)
- **problem-frame**: Produce a concise, structured scientific problem frame from a user request. Use when an agent needs to clarify the research objective, narrow scope, surface ambiguities, extract key variables and constraints, and define evidence-based success criteria before literature review, hypothesis generation, or experiment planning. ([ustcllm/kaivu-agent-ts](https://github.com/ustcllm/kaivu-agent-ts/tree/HEAD/examples/problem-frame-skill) · ★3 · 2 copies)
- **problem-frame**: Structure a fuzzy client problem into pain, sub-problems, constraints, stakeholders, unknowns, and a canvas. ([casper7995/festack](https://github.com/casper7995/festack/tree/HEAD/skills/problem-frame))
